Compare expansion of air on warming

CL-0066 · VisionariesAI Labs Private Limited · Virtual practical

Aim

Compare matched closed-air references with a free moving boundary and a rigid fixed volume. Observe warming and cooling; distinguish gas and jacket temperature, volume and absolute pressure.

Principle and apparatus

Idealized diatomic-air reference:PV=nRT with temperature in kelvin and pressure absolute. Frictionless quasistatic piston, rigid-volume comparison, thermostatted annular jackets, volume scale, calculated thermometers and absolute-pressure gauge. The chosen common conductance gives different process time constants because Cp=3.5R and Cv=2.5R.

Procedure

Record initial volume, absolute pressure, gas temperature, jacket target and chosen conductance. Predict the two boundary responses. Record both starting states, then run and pause at matched elapsed times. Compare heating, cooling, equal-temperature and return-jacket programmes. At120s in the return programme the jacket changes, while gas temperature remains continuous. Record gas temperature, volume, pressure, heat, work and retained mass. Apply substitutions with units, use kelvin ratios, checkPV=nRT andQ−W=ΔU, then write result and limitations.

Prediction

A moving boundary can expand; a rigid one changes absolute pressure. Compare gas, not jacket, temperatures. <script>

Formula substitutions and worked results

Trial 1: Free-piston reference

  1. Initial absolute temperature

    T₀ = θ₀+273.15

    Substitution: 25.000000+273.15

    Result: 298.150000 K

  2. Selected jacket absolute temperature

    Tj,target = θj,target+273.15

    Substitution: 65.000000+273.15

    Result: 338.150000 K

  3. Closed gas amount

    n = P₀(V₀/1000)/(RT₀)

    Substitution: 101.325000 × (50.000000/1000)/(8.314462618 × 298.150000)

    Result: 0.002043702226 mol; retained unchanged

  4. Process molar heat capacity

    Cp = 3.5R

    Substitution: 3.5 × 8.314462618

    Result: 29.100619 J/(mol K); chosen ideal-diatomic reference

  5. Process thermal capacity

    C = n Cprocess

    Substitution: 0.002043702226 × 29.100619

    Result: 0.05947300017 J/K

  6. Thermal time constant

    τ = C/G

    Substitution: 0.05947300017/0.002

    Result: 29.736500 s; G is chosen, not measured calibration

  7. Total gas temperature change

    ΔTtotal = Tgas−T₀

    Substitution: 298.150000−298.150000

    Result: 0 K; stable exponential residual retained

  8. Current gas absolute temperature

    Tgas = T₀+ΔTtotal

    Substitution: 298.150000+(0)

    Result: 298.150000 K

  9. Current gas Celsius temperature

    θgas = Tgas−273.15

    Substitution: 298.150000−273.15

    Result: 25.000000 °C

  10. Current jacket temperature

    θj = current prescribed jacket K−273.15

    Substitution: 338.150000−273.15

    Result: 65.000000 °C; not assumed equal to gas temperature

  11. Volume change

    ΔV = V₀ ΔTtotal/T₀

    Substitution: 50.000000 × (0)/298.150000

    Result: 0 mL

  12. Current gas volume

    V = V₀+ΔV

    Substitution: 50.000000+(0)

    Result: 50.000000 mL

  13. Absolute-pressure change

    ΔP = 0 for imposed constant pressure

    Substitution: Frictionless quasistatic moving boundary

    Result: 0 kPa

  14. Current absolute pressure

    P = P₀+ΔP

    Substitution: 101.325000+(0)

    Result: 101.325000 kPa; absolute, not gauge pressure

  15. Net heat into gas

    Qnet = n Cprocess ΔTtotal

    Substitution: 0.002043702226 × 29.100619 × (0)

    Result: 0 J; sum of signed interval heat

  16. Net work by gas

    Wnet = P₀ ΔV/1000

    Substitution: 101.325000 × (0)/1000

    Result: 0 J; kPa mL/1000 converts to J

  17. Net internal-energy change

    ΔU = n Cv ΔTtotal

    Substitution: 0.002043702226 × 20.786157 × (0)

    Result: 0 J

  18. Current heat-flow rate

    Q̇ = G(Tj−Tgas)

    Substitution: 0.002 × (338.150000−298.150000)

    Result: 0.08 W; sign changes immediately when jacket switches

  19. Current gas-temperature rate

    dT/dt = Q̇/(n Cprocess)

    Substitution: 0.08/0.05947300017

    Result: 1.345148 K/s

  20. Closed gas mass

    m = n M ×1000

    Substitution: 0.002043702226 × 0.02897 ×1000

    Result: 0.05920605349 g; chosen mean molar mass

  21. Gas density

    ρ = m/(V/1000)

    Substitution: 0.05920605349/(50.000000/1000)

    Result: 1.184121 g/L

  22. Gas-column height

    h = V/A128

    Substitution: 50.000000/4.906767

    Result: 10.190008 cm; actual polygonal area

  23. Pressure-volume energy

    PV = P(V/1000)

    Substitution: 101.325000 × (50.000000/1000)

    Result: 5.066250 J

  24. Ideal-gas energy expression

    nRT = n R Tgas

    Substitution: 0.002043702226 × 8.314462618 × 298.150000

    Result: 5.066250 J; equals PV

  25. First-law check

    Qnet−Wnet−ΔU

    Substitution: 0−(0)−(0)

    Result: 0 J; floating-point residual near zero

  26. Molecular rms speed reference

    vrms = √(3RTgas/M)

    Substitution: √(3 × 8.314462618 × 298.150000/0.02897)

    Result: 506.664970 m/s; not visible piston speed or bulk flow

Trial 2: Rigid-volume comparison

  1. Initial absolute temperature

    T₀ = θ₀+273.15

    Substitution: 25.000000+273.15

    Result: 298.150000 K

  4. Process molar heat capacity

  5. Process thermal capacity

    Result: 0.04248071441 J/K

  6. Thermal time constant

    τ = C/G

    Substitution: 0.04248071441/0.002

    Result: 21.240357 s; G is chosen, not measured calibration

  7. Total gas temperature change

    ΔTtotal = Tgas−T₀

    Substitution: 298.150000−298.150000

    Result: 0 K; stable exponential residual retained

  8. Current gas absolute temperature

    Tgas = T₀+ΔTtotal

    Substitution: 298.150000+(0)

    Result: 298.150000 K

  9. Current gas Celsius temperature

    θgas = Tgas−273.15

    Substitution: 298.150000−273.15

    Result: 25.000000 °C

  10. Current jacket temperature

  23. Net heat into gas

    Qnet = n Cprocess ΔTtotal

    Substitution: 0.002810192946 × 20.786157 × (50.000000)

    Result: 2.920656 J; sum of signed interval heat

  24. Net work by gas

    Wnet = 0 at fixed volume

    Substitution: ΔV=0

    Result: 0 J; kPa mL/1000 converts to J

  25. Net internal-energy change

    ΔU = n Cv ΔTtotal

    Substitution: 0.002810192946 × 20.786157 × (50.000000)

    Result: 2.920656 J

  26. Current heat-flow rate

    Q̇ = G(Tj−Tgas)

    Substitution: 0.005 × (358.150000−358.150000)

    Result: 1.76044068e-12 W; sign changes immediately when jacket switches

  27. Current gas-temperature rate

    dT/dt = Q̇/(n Cprocess)

    Substitution: 1.76044068e-12/0.0584131105

    Result: 3.01377665e-11 K/s

  28. Closed gas mass

    m = n M ×1000

    Substitution: 0.002810192946 × 0.02897 ×1000

    Result: 0.08141128965 g; chosen mean molar mass

  29. Gas density

    ρ = m/(V/1000)

    Substitution: 0.08141128965/(60.000000/1000)

    Result: 1.356855 g/L

  30. Gas-column height

    h = V/A128

    Substitution: 60.000000/4.906767

    Result: 12.228010 cm; actual polygonal area

  31. Pressure-volume energy

    PV = P(V/1000)

    Substitution: 139.471037 × (60.000000/1000)

    Result: 8.368262 J

  32. Ideal-gas energy expression

    nRT = n R Tgas

    Substitution: 0.002810192946 × 8.314462618 × 358.150000

    Result: 8.368262 J; equals PV

  33. First-law check

    Qnet−Wnet−ΔU

    Substitution: 2.920656−(0)−(2.920656)

    Result: 0 J; floating-point residual near zero

  34. Molecular rms speed reference

    vrms = √(3RTgas/M)

    Substitution: √(3 × 8.314462618 × 358.150000/0.02897)

    Result: 555.310583 m/s; not visible piston speed or bulk flow

Result, conclusion and limitations

Use kelvin, keep the trapped amount fixed, and account for signed heat/work during return cooling. <img src=x> Chosen virtual reference.

Closed idealized dry diatomic air, fixed reference heat capacities and mean molar mass, uniform gas temperature, chosen lumped conductance and externally prescribed jacket. Moving boundary is frictionless and quasistatic; no piston inertia/friction/leaks, real-gas corrections, humidity, evaporation, spatial temperature fields, jacket/hardware thermal storage or measured apparatus calibration. Jacket stays below the piston face; no varying water-head load. Rod/probe volumes do not intrude into gas. Air colour, if displayed, is an explanatory overlay. Finite return cooling approaches the initial state rather than snapping to it. This virtual model is not pressure-vessel certification or a physical sealed-container heating procedure. Class placement is editorial; quantitative energy calculations are teacher extensions.
